1 - 欢迎

大家好，欢迎来到《无监督深度学习、GAN 与变分自编码器》课程。

这节课我们会先介绍课程的主要内容，你将会学到什么，以及课程的整体结构是怎么安排的。

那么，这门课到底是讲什么的呢？

嗯，就如标题所说的那样。

它讲的是无监督深度学习，讲的是 GAN，也讲变分自编码器。

这是什么意思呢？

首先，GAN 和变分自编码器都属于无监督深度学习的具体例子。

之所以说它们是“无监督”的，是因为它们不需要标签就能学习。

这一点非常重要。

为什么这么重要呢？

因为世界上绝大多数的数据都是没有标签的。

举个例子，互联网上的大部分数据，并不会附带结构清晰的标签。

更何况，给数据打标签是个既昂贵又极其耗时的过程。

总得有人来做这件事，而做这件事的人就得拿报酬，除非你打算自己动手干。

不过，无监督学习本身也非常酷。

通过无监督学习，我们能够找出数据中潜藏的结构。

比如说，我们知道了怎么把“一”变成“七”，再把“七”变成“九”。

我们也明白了，想要调整人脸的某些特征时，该去转动哪个“旋钮”。

举个例子，我们可以取一张人脸，让它看起来更老或更年轻。

由此可见，我们找到了控制年龄的特征。

我们可以取一张人脸，让它微笑或皱眉。

由此可见，我们找到了控制面部表情的特征。

我们可以给一张人脸加上眼镜、胡须、不同颜色的头发等等。

那么，我们来说说这门课的结构是如何安排的。

在这门课里，你将学到无监督深度学习的基础知识。

整个课程分为三个阶段。

第一阶段，我们会回顾一下不使用深度学习的生成式建模。

这算是贝叶斯网络和隐变量模型的一个小型回顾。

为了把这些概念应用到真实数据上，我们会运用变分高斯混合模型。

这在普通的 GMM 基础上，加入了贝叶斯机器学习和变分推断。

变分推断会将我们引向下一个主题，届时我们会学习变分自编码器。

变分自编码器听上去可能和普通自编码器差不多，但实际上，它们差别很大。

在这门课里，你就会明白为什么它们如此强大，又为何能在深度学习领域产生如此深远的影响。

第三阶段，我们将聚焦 GAN，也就是生成对抗网络。

著名的深度学习先驱 Yan Lacun 对 GAN 有过这样的评价。

他说，这是过去十年机器学习领域里最有趣的想法。

在生成逼真、可信的图像和声音方面，GAN 是最强大的技术。

它们催生了像 thispersondoesnotexist.com 和 whichfacesreal.com 这样的项目，我非常推荐你去访问一下，亲身感受 GAN 有多么强大。

我建议你去 whichfacesreal.com 看看，在那里你可以玩一个游戏，试着猜猜两张图片里哪张是真的，哪张是假的。

我想你会认同我的看法：要分辨出哪张脸是真的、哪张是计算机生成的，几乎是不可能的。

现在，我得强调一下，这门课里的所有内容都非常注重动手实践。

我们绝不会只讨论概念而不去用代码实现它。

因此，你可以期待在这门课里进行大量的编程练习。

这门课会用到 Theano 或 TensorFlow，用哪个都行。

理想情况下，你可以选择自己用着最顺手的库，不管是什么都行。

如果你喜欢 PyTorch 或别的什么，也尽管用，没关系。

请记住，这些深度学习概念说到底只是概念。

任何库都能够实现这些概念，前提是你会用那个库。

希望这节课让你对这门课的内容以及你将学到什么，有了一个大致的了解。

希望你也和我一样，对 GAN 和无监督深度学习那令人惊叹的能力印象深刻。

同时，也希望你对学习这些前沿概念的理论与实现倍感期待。

感谢收听，我们下节课再见。

英文原文

Hey everyone and welcome to Unsupervised Deep Learning, GANs and Variational Autoencoders.

In this lecture, we'll be talking about what this course is about, what you can expect to learn, and how this course is structured.

So what is this course about?

Well, it's just like the title says.

It's about unsupervised deep learning, it's about GANs, and it's about variational autoencoders.

So what does this mean?

Firstly, GANs and variational autoencoders are examples of unsupervised deep learning.

They are unsupervised because they don't require labels to learn.

This is very important.

Why is this important?

Because most of the data in the world is not labeled.

For example, most of the data on the internet does not come with nice structured labels.

Furthermore, labeling data is a very expensive and time-consuming process.

Somebody has to do it, and that person has to get paid unless you want to do it yourself.

But unsupervised learning by itself is very cool.

Using unsupervised learning, we've been able to figure out the underlying structure in data.

For example, we know how to transform ones into sevens and sevens into nines.

We know which knobs to turn when we want to tweak certain features of faces.

For example, we can take a face and make it look older or younger.

Therefore, we have learned which feature controls age.

We can take a face and make it smile or frown.

Therefore, we have learned which feature controls facial expression.

We can take a face and add glasses, beards, different colored hair, and more.

So let's talk about how this course will be structured.

In this course, you will learn the fundamentals of unsupervised deep learning.

This course is split up into three phases.

In the first phase, we will review generative modeling without deep learning.

This is like a mini review of Bayesian networks and latent variable models.

To apply these concepts to real data, we will apply the variational Gaussian mixture model.

This adds Bayesian machine learning and variational inference to the vanilla GMM.

Variational inference brings us to our next topic, where we will study variational autoencoders.

Variational autoencoders might sound like regular autoencoders, but in fact, they are very different.

In this course, you will learn why they are so powerful and why they made such a big impact in deep learning.

In the third phase, we will look at GANs, or generative adversarial networks.

The famous deep learning pioneer Yan Lacun had this to say about GANs.

He said they were the most interesting idea in the past 10 years in machine learning.

Gans are the most powerful technique when it comes to generating realistic and believable images and sounds.

They've spawned projects like thispersondoesnotexist.com and which facesreal.com, both of which I highly recommend you visit to get a taste of just how powerful GANs can be.

I recommend going to whichfacesreal.com, where you get to play a game to try and guess which out of the two images is real and which is fake.

I think you'll agree with me that it's nearly impossible to tell which faces are real and which faces have been generated by a computer.

Now I have to mention that everything in this course is extremely hands-on.

We will never discuss a concept without also implementing that concept in code.

Therefore, you can expect to do lots of coding in this course.

This course uses Theano or TensorFlow, either of which is fine.

Ideally, you can choose whichever library you are most comfortable in, whatever that may be.

If you like PyTorch or something else, you can feel free to use that too.

Remember, these deep learning concepts are just concepts.

Any library is capable of implementing these concepts provided that you know how to use that library.

So I hope this lecture gave you some idea of what this course is about and what you will learn.

I hope that you are just as impressed as I am about the amazing capabilities of GANs and unsupervised deep learning.

And I hope you're excited about learning both the theory and implementation of these state-of-the-art concepts.

Thanks for listening and I'll see you in the next lecture.

2 - 本课程在深度学习学习体系中的定位

大家好，欢迎回到《无监督深度学习》第二部分。

这节课，我要回答一个几乎每天都有人问我的问题。

这门课在我整体的深度学习学习路径中，该放在哪个位置呢？

深度学习是个广阔且在不断发展的领域，所以，从中挑选出哪些部分跟你当前想学的内容相关，就显得很重要，因为想一口气学完所有东西是不现实的。

那么，我们先从这门课说起。

这门课讲的是生成式建模，会用到的工具有变分自编码器和 GAN。

通常，我们把这称为无监督深度学习。

同时，这也是无监督深度学习的第二部分。

那无监督深度学习第一部分又讲了什么呢？

嗯，在无监督深度学习第一部分中，我们关注的焦点不同，不是生成式建模，而是学习隐式表征，以此来辅助监督学习。

所以你在那儿学到的技术，在这里不会直接用到，但你会在一些概念上打下更扎实的基础，这虽非必需，却很有用。

你可能也注意到了，我还有另一门关于无监督的课程，但那门课讲的是通用的无监督机器学习，并非深度学习。

那门课的重点是聚类，尤其是从概率的视角来看待聚类。

其中也提到过隐式表征，这对我们现在的学习会很有帮助。

我们在那门课里学过的一个重要模型，高斯混合模型，将会在这门课中再次出现。

不过，只需掌握其核心思想即可，并且在这门课里我们会用 scikit-learn 来实现。

所以，还是一样，那门课不学也行，但学了会很有用。

那么，学习这门课有哪些重要的先修知识要求呢？

嗯，首先，你应该理解神经网络的结构、它的代价函数，以及它的训练算法，也就是所谓的反向传播或梯度下降。

这些内容在《深度学习》第一部分中讲过。

现在，为了搭建更复杂的神经网络，我们需要利用自动微分，这样就无需手动推导梯度下降的方程式了。

能帮我们做到这点的库有 Theano 和 TensorFlow，我们在本课程中两个都会用到。

实际上，本课程所有内容都会同步用 Theano 和 TensorFlow 来实现，所以哪怕你只会其中一个，也没问题。

而 Theano 和 TensorFlow 在《深度学习》第二部分中讲过了。

这还没完。

GAN 结构的一个较新发展是引入了卷积。

卷积本身是个重要的概念，所以你最好多做些练习，好让自己熟悉卷积到底能做什么。

但这并非我们第一次在神经网络中使用卷积，它对于像图像、声音甚至文本这类连续或时变信号非常有用。

确实，这些恰恰就是我们在这门课中想要生成的东西。

我们在《深度学习》第三部分中讲过了卷积神经网络。

在这门课里，我们也会稍微提及强化学习和博弈论。

虽然我确实有讲强化学习的课程，但可以比较肯定地说，你完全不需要了解那方面的知识，也能掌握本课所教的内容。

不过，就像所有机器学习领域一样，你知道得越多，就越能把新知识和旧知识联系起来，自然也会学得越得心应手。

所以，你看，走到这一步可谓是一段漫长的旅程。

你能在一天之内就学会这些东西吗？

大概不能。

因此，如果你想确保自己理解本课程涵盖的主题，就请务必在我刚才讨论的那些主题上打好坚实的基础。

那你非得从我的课程里学这些基础吗？

不一定，但这是一种保证你能全面覆盖知识点的好方法。

有时，人们会上一些课，用漂亮的动画和比喻来解释深度学习的原理。

问题在于，你是否把理解这些比喻，等同于真正的理解。

不幸的是，这会导致许多困惑。

所以，无论你从哪儿学，请确保自己不只是停留在理解比喻的层面，因为遗憾的是，比喻并不能转化为实际可运行的代码。

英文原文

Hey everyone and welcome back to this class, Unsupervised Deep Learning Part 2.

In this lecture, I'm going to answer a question I receive almost every day.

Where does this course fit into my deep learning studies?

Deep learning is a vast and growing field, so it's important to pick and choose which parts of it are relevant to what you currently want to learn about, since learning it all at once would be impossible.

So let's start with this course.

This course is about generative modeling, using tools like the variational autoencoder and the GAN.

In general, we call this unsupervised deep learning.

At the same time, this is unsupervised deep learning part two.

So where does that leave unsupervised deep learning part one?

Well, in unsupervised deep learning part one, we had a different focus, not generative modeling, but learning latent representations in order to facilitate supervised learning.

So the techniques you learn there are not directly applied here, but you would have gotten a more solid foundation on a few concepts, not required, but useful.

You may have also noticed that I have yet another unsupervised course, but this is on unsupervised machine learning in general, not deep learning.

The focus in this course was clustering, and in particular a probabilistic perspective on clustering.

This also had some mention of latent representations, so that would be useful for us here.

One important model we learned about in that course, the Gaussian mixture model, will appear again in this course.

However, only the main ideas are important, and in this course we'll be using scikit-learn.

So again, not required, but useful.

So what are some important prerequisites for this course?

Well, firstly, you should understand the architecture of a neural network, its cost function, and its training algorithm, which is called backpropagation or gradient descent.

This was covered in Deep Learning Part 1.

Now, in order to build more complex neural networks, we need to take advantage of automatic differentiation so that we don't have to derive gradient descent equations by hand.

Some libraries that help us do this are Theano and TensorFlow, both of which we'll be using in this class.

Now everything we do in this course will be in Theano and TensorFlow in parallel, so if you only know one of these, that's fine.

So Theano and TensorFlow were covered in Deep Learning Part 2.

Now that's not all.

One of the more recent developments in GAN architectures was the inclusion of convolution.

Convolution by itself is an important concept, and so you want to do lots of examples so you can familiarize yourself with what convolution does.

But this is not the first time we've included convolution in our neural networks, which is useful for continuous or time-varying signals like images, sound, and even text.

Indeed, these are the very things we are interested in generating in this course.

We covered convolutional neural networks in Deep Learning Part 3.

In this course, we make some references to reinforcement learning and game theory as well.

And while I do have courses on reinforcement learning, it's probably safe to say you don't need to know anything about it to grasp what is taught in this course.

However, like all machine learning, the more you know, the more you can relate what you know to other things you know, and the more comfortable you will be.

So, you see that it's been a long journey to get to this point.

Are you going to learn about these things in a day?

Probably not.

So, if you want to make sure you understand the topics covered in this course, make sure you have a solid foundation in the topics I just discussed.

Do you have to learn them from my courses?

Not necessarily, but it's a good way to ensure you have solid coverage.

Sometimes, people take courses that give you nice animations and metaphors for how things work in deep learning.

The problem is if you equate understanding these metaphors to actual understanding.

Unfortunately, this will lead to a lot of confusion.

So, learn these things from wherever you want, but make sure you're not just learning metaphors, because unfortunately, metaphors do not translate into working code.

3 - 代码与数据获取方式

大家好，欢迎回到《无监督深度学习》第二部分。

这节课，我会跟大家说一下课程代码的获取方式。

跟往常一样，这门课的代码可以从我的 GitHub 主页下载，地址是 github.com 斜杠 lazyprogrammer 斜杠 machine learning examples。

获取代码时，请不要尝试从网页上直接复制粘贴，而要用 Git 来操作。

我以前见过有学生这么做，结果经常会出现各种错误。

所以，直接用 git clone 这个命令，后面加上我的 GitHub 地址就行。

跟这门课相关的文件夹是 unsupervised class 三。

接下来，课程要用到的数据从哪儿获取呢？

在这门课里，我们开始时会用 MNIST 数据集来测试算法。你也知道，有这样一个基准数据集很有帮助，因为现阶段重要的是回答这个问题：这和我已知的其他算法有什么不同？

要有效回答这个问题，你用的数据集应该固定不变。

如果你上过我之前的任何一门课，很可能这个数据集已经在正确的位置上了。

如果还没有，那就按下面的步骤来做。

去访问 Kaggle.com 斜杠 C 斜杠 digit recognizer。

你会看到这个页面。

点开 data 链接。

下载 train.csv。

下载好 train.csv 之后，你要把它放到一个叫 large files 的文件夹里，这个文件夹跟课程文件夹同级。

如果这个文件夹还不存在，那就新建一个。

所有代码都默认 MNIST 在这个位置，所以请务必确认放对了地方。

到了课程后面，我们会看到一些更复杂的数据集，特别是名人面部图像。

这些数据代码会自动下载，所以你不需要手动去下，当然你想手动下也行。

请注意，这是一个高达一 GB 的压缩文件，下载后还需要解压，所以下载过程会比较漫长，脚本可能会看起来像卡住了一样。

或者，你也可以自己把文件下载并解压到 large files 文件夹中。

最后，我想借此机会明确说明一下，本课程中的每一个编程练习，都是设计出来让你先自己尝试去完成的。

到了这个阶段，你应该能不需要太多帮助，直接从理论过渡到代码实现了。

当然，你可能得翻几次文档，不过这都算是编程时的家常便饭了。

等你真正自己尝试着去实现了代码之后，再去看讲解视频或 GitHub 上的代码。

关于这些理念，附录课程《如何靠自己写代码》里有更深入的讨论，如果你想听更详细的解释，可以去看看那节。

我总是建议我的学生尽量自己动手写代码，但如果你卡住了或者需要帮助，GitHub 仓库里的代码是已经验证过可以运行的。

所以你随时可以用它来对照检查，要是卡住了，也随时可以通过答疑区提问。

英文原文

Hey everyone and welcome back to this class, Unsupervised Deep Learning, Part 2.

In this lecture, I'm going to discuss where to get the code for this course.

So as usual, the code in this course can be downloaded from my GitHub page, github.com slash lazyprogrammer slash machine learning examples.

In order to get the code, please don't try to copy and paste it from the web page itself and use Git instead.

I've seen students do this in the past and it frequently causes errors.

So just use the command git clone and then my GitHub URL.

The relevant folder for this course is unsupervised class 3.

Next, where can you get the data for this course?

In this course, we will be using the MNIST dataset initially to test our algorithms. As you know, it helps to have a benchmark like this because what's important at this point is answering the question, how is this different from the other algorithms I know?

And to answer that effectively, your data set should be a fixed constant.

If you've taken any of my previous courses, you most likely already have this data set in the correct place.

If not, here's what you do.

Go to Kaggle.com slash C slash digit recognizer.

You'll see this page.

Go to the data link.

Download train.csv.

Once you have train.csv, you want to put it in a folder called large files adjacent to the class folder.

If this folder doesn't exist yet, create it.

All the code expects MNIST to be in this spot, so make sure that's where it is.

Later in the course, we'll be looking at some more complicated data sets, specifically images of celebrity faces.

This will be downloaded automatically by the code, so you won't have to download anything manually, although you could if you wanted to.

Note that this is a huge one gigabyte zip file that also needs to be unzipped, so the download process will be lengthy and the script might seem like it's not doing anything.

Alternatively, you can just download and unzip the files yourself into the large files folder.

Finally, I want to take this opportunity to state explicitly that every coding exercise in this course is meant as an exercise for you to do by yourself first.

At this level, you should be able to go directly from theory to code without much help.

Of course, you may have to refer to the documentation a few times, but that's just business as usual when you're programming.

Once you've made an honest attempt at implementing the code yourself, then watch the videos or look at the code on GitHub.

These ideas are discussed more in depth in the appendix lecture, How to Code by Yourself, so you may want to watch that for a more elaborate explanation.

I always recommend my students try to code for themselves, but if you get stuck or you need help, then the code in the GitHub repo has already been verified to work.

So you can always check your code against that, and you can always ask questions using the QA if you get stuck.

4 - 如何在本课程中取得优异成绩

这一讲里，我想给你几条能帮你在这门课中取得成功的实用小建议。

一般来说，我发现只要学生遵循这些建议，就一定能学好。

而很多时候，看到那些进展不顺的学生，原因往往都是没有按这些建议来做。

首先我想说的是，我真心相信任何人都能在这门课里学有所成。

不论你是还在上高中的青少年，还是已经退休、几十年没碰过大学数学的长者，情况都是这样。

各种情况归根结底，都取决于你是否遵循指导。

勤奋是关键。

只要你能对自己做出承诺，保证勤奋并遵循这些准则，那我就能向你承诺，你一定能成功。

那么，这些建议具体是什么呢？

长话短说，第一条建议就是用好答疑区。

我常常看到有学生留言说：“哎呀，这课里怎么缺了这个那个。”我的回复通常是：“呃，其实这个在第五讲里讲过了。”你只是看漏了而已。

可你瞧，你不问的话，我是没法告诉你这些的。

所以，哪种结果更好呢？

是你这辈子都不知道问题的答案，还是你干脆开口问，哪怕会伤到一点点自尊，但至少能增长知识和技能？

我更偏向那个能让你变得更优秀、更有见识的结果。

有时候，学生会抗拒这条建议，说：“但我不知道要问什么。”这就对了。

你正是最应该提问的那类人。

你不知道怎么组织问题的原因，是你对这个主题思考得还不够透彻。

多数时候，就我所见，在尝试提出问题的过程中，这个问题往往自己就解答了。

好了，第二条建议是，要满足这门课程的先修要求。

很多学生把先修要求看作是一种准入门槛。

他们会想：“哎呀，讲师一提先修要求，就是要我做更多功课来惩罚我。”“他是在暗示我连这都不懂，很蠢。”各位，不是这样的。

先修要求只是一些用来帮助你的信息。

它就只是信息。

不是障碍。

更不是任何要攻击你的东西。

它就只是信息而已。

那这些信息怎么帮你呢？

是这样，有了这个信息之后，如果你在课里看到任何觉得“咦，这看起来好怪，我从没见过”的东西。

这时，就是个重温先修内容的好时机了，看看自己是不是遗漏了什么。

要是没有这份先修列表，你就会毫无头绪，不知从何查起。

但有了这份列表，你就有了一个指南，能告诉你可能还欠缺什么。

好，现在来看第三条建议。

这一点我已经提过了，但因为实在太重要，值得再重复一遍。

在这门课里，要积极参与，亲自动手实践。

就像，如果你在上这门课的时候，手没有实实在在地动起来，那你学习的方式就是错的。

这门课主要有两类：概念讲解类和编程实践类。

上概念讲解课时，你必须动手做手写笔记。

我自己过去的体会、许多其他人的说法，以及大量心理学研究都表明，手写笔记是一种更高效的学习和吸收信息的方式。

这比死盯着幻灯片要好，比被动看视频要好，甚至比打字记笔记都要好。

动手书写，似乎能在头脑和肌肉间建立更强大的连接。

至于编程实践课，这点你肯定早就知道了。

但还是要打开你最喜欢的编程环境，把那些代码写下来。

如果你上的是学习算法的课，那请记住，亲手实现算法就是你的课后练习。

如果你只是把讲座从头看到尾，光在那儿摆弄手指，那你就等于是在自己做完练习之前，先偷看了答案，这算是一种作弊。

无论你是在实现算法，还是仅仅在学习某个库的用法，你都必须实际去写代码。

我不认识任何不写代码的数据科学家或机器学习工程师。

我想说明一下，这张建议列表其实是“如何取得成功”那堂完整课的浓缩版，你可以在本课程的常见问题答疑区找到它。

所以，如果你对这些建议有任何异议，或者觉得它们不太合理，请去看那节课了解更详细的内容。

好了，那所有这些建议里的共同点是什么呢？

共同点就是，学生们之所以不遵循这些建议，是因为他们被自我挡住了路。

事实上，自我是阻碍你在这门课上取得成功的头号原因。

你的骄傲正在阻碍你在这门课上取得成功。

说个有趣的现象：我注意到，有相当大比例声称自己拥有博士学位的学生，表现却非常糟糕。

而我却看到本科生、硕士生，甚至是高中生做得更好。

这怎么可能呢？

嗯，你看，博士们的问题是，他们觉得自己太聪明了，没什么可学的了。

这就是自我和骄傲阻碍他们学习新东西的一个例子。

就第一条建议而言，自我阻碍了人们去使用答疑区。

用答疑区，常常意味着你要提出问题，这会暴露你的错误假设，然后你的错误会由回答你问题的人来纠正。

知道自己弄错了某些东西，这可能会让你有点受伤。

但就像我刚才说的，认识到自己错了，总比一直错下去还盲目无知要好。

就第二条建议而言，自我阻碍了人们去满足先修要求。

他们会想：“我学过微积分，那自然就懂微积分，以后看到任何跟微积分沾边的东西，我肯定也能理解，不然就是对方没讲明白。”各位，这可不是个好的人生态度。

尽管我十几年前就上了第一堂机器学习课，但直到今天，我还时常能学到关于机器学习的新东西。

我们也说清楚点：如果你在一门课里拿了个B或C，也就是所谓的平均分，那从分数上看，你的理解程度其实只有百分之六七十。

我看到的另一个常见借口是：“哎呀，没错，我是二十年前学过微积分，但早忘光了。”“现在你有责任重新教我了。”各位，这不对，这可不叫满足了先修要求。

请记住，先修要求的存在，是为了确保你具备一项你能用得上的技能。

它不是一份资格证明列表。

资格证明在你去应聘时也许有用，但在一个自定进度的课程里，当你唯一能依靠的就是自己时，这些就无关紧要了。

不妨这样想：如果你有一张纸，上面写着你“会开飞机”，但实际上，你早把怎么开飞机忘得一干二净了，你还敢去开吗？

我觉得那会非常危险，而且我相信，任何一个正常人，对这个问题都不会回答“敢”。

最后来看第三条建议，自我阻碍了人们去亲自动手实践、做笔记和写代码。

我能这么说，是因为当我提到写代码是个很好的练习，而且这其实就是你做数据科学家时要做的工作时，有些人宁肯和我争辩，也不愿接受这个建议。

他们更在乎骂我、威胁我，而不是真正去提升自己，成为更出色的数据科学家。

他们就是无法接受这样一个事实：肌肉记忆是真实存在的，写代码是有效的练习，而且写代码就是这份工作的本质。

他们宁愿在心里相信自己无需任何练习，就能写出完美的代码。

他们害怕写代码的原因在于，一旦开始写，他们就可能犯错，这会暴露出他们并不完美的编程能力。

而对于一个自尊心太强、自我太大的人来说，这是最难承受的。

他们就是无法面对自己犯错或做错事的可能性。

这负担重到让他们无法承受。

好，希望各位能明白我的意思。

别让你的自我，成为你成功路上的绊脚石。

别害怕犯错，然后尽最大努力，勤勤恳恳地遵循这些指导。

感谢你的聆听，我们下节课再见。

5 - 采样的含义是什么

大家好，欢迎回到《无监督深度学习》第二部分。

这节课，我们来讨论一下从已学习的模型中进行采样意味着什么。

我们之前已经见过基础意义上的采样是怎么做的了。

比如，从高斯分布中采样，或是从伯努利分布中采样，这就像抛硬币，结果永远是零或一。

在接下来的几节课里，我们要再进一步。

我们打算构建一个贝叶斯分类器。

记住，贝叶斯分类器是一个生成式模型。

这意味着，对于每个类别 y，我们学习的是给定 y 下 x 的分布 p，而不是直接对给定 x 下 y 的分布 p 进行建模。

而“生成式”这个名字实际上非常贴切，因为你稍后会看到，我们将用这个模型来生成数据。

现在，学习这样一个分布，方法有很多，实际上多得是。

不过，一个标准的方法是直接拿一个高斯分布去拟合数据。

所以，要找到给定 y 下 x 的分布 p，我们给每个 y 需要做的就是，先找出所有属于这个类别的 x。

下一步是计算它们的均值和协方差，并保存下来。

你可能还记得，我们可以基于贝叶斯规则来做分类决策，取给定 x 下 y 的分布 p 的 argmax，这个后验概率等于给定 y 下 x 的分布 p 乘以 y 的分布 p 再除以 x 的分布 p。

然而，在这门课里，我们实际上对准确率不感兴趣，因此我们不需要做任何预测。

在这门课里，我们感兴趣的是采样。

那么，从这个模型中进行采样，有两种方法。

顺便说一句，这一切可能看起来微不足道，但这些概念后面会再次出现，所以你最好把他们牢记在心。

那么，第一种采样方法是直接选一个类别。

假设我们想要类别 y 等于一。

现在，我们知道给定 y 等于一时 x 的分布 p 是一个高斯分布，而我们也知道如何从高斯分布中采样。

因此，我们可以用 psi pi 很轻松地从这个高斯分布中抽取一个样本。

第二种采样方法，操作上同样简单，但概念上稍有不同。

你可以把我们的模型想象成一个图模型。

每个节点都是一个不同的分布。

具体来说，我们有 y 的分布 p 和给定 y 下 x 的分布 p。

这张图把 x 和 y 之间的依赖关系展示得非常清楚。

我们可以看到，y 对 x 长什么样有影响。

这很合理。

如果 y 等于一，那么我们生成的图像看起来就会和 y 等于八时的图像大不相同。

但关键在于，y 有其自身的分布。

它是一个类别分布，或者说离散分布。

要学习 y 的分布 p，我们只需统计每个类别图像在训练数据中出现的次数，然后除以训练样本总数。

但正因为它本身就是一个分布，这意味着我们也可以从中采样。

换句话说，我们谈到的第一种采样方法，仅仅是从一个给定的高斯分布中采样，也就是在 psi pi 里调用一个函数。

我们的采样函数会接收一个参数 y，以便我们决定从哪个高斯分布中采样。

第二种采样方法在概念上是不同的。

我们现在是从联合分布 p(x, y) 中进行采样。

由于 x 依赖于 y，我们先从先验 y 的分布 p 中采样，这可以从数据中学到，然后我们再从给定 y 下 x 的分布 p 中采样，这一步我们之前已经知道怎么做了。

好，我想这些细节足够你自己去实现一些东西了。

到了这一步，我假设你已经下载好了数据，并且知道如何用 NumPy 来加载它。

所以，接下来的练习就是，编写一个贝叶斯分类器，它能拟合数据，并用我们刚刚讨论的两种方法从模型中生成样本。

一种是基于给定的 y 进行采样，也就是从给定 y 下 x 的分布 p 中采样；另一种是从联合分布 p(x, y) 中采样。

英文原文

Hey everyone, and welcome back to this class, Unsupervised Deep Learning, Part 2.

In this lecture, we are going to discuss what it means to sample from a learned model.

We've already seen how to draw samples in the basic sense.

For example, sampling from a Gaussian distribution or sampling from a Bernoulli distribution, which is like a coin toss, so the result is always a zero or a one.

In the coming lectures, we are going to take this one step further.

Our plan is to build a Bayes classifier.

Remember that a Bayes classifier is a generative model.

That means for each class y, we are learning the distribution p of x given y rather than directly modeling p of y given x.

And the name generative is actually very appropriate in this case because as you'll see later, we are going to use this model to generate data.

Now, there are multiple ways to learn such a distribution, many ways in fact.

However, a standard method is to simply fit a Gaussian to the data.

So, to find p of x given y, all we need to do for each y is first find all the x's that belong to this class.

The next step is to take their mean and their covariance and save them.

Now, as you may remember, we can make a classification decision based on Bayes' rule, taking the argmax of p of y given x, which is equal to p of x given y times p of y divided by p of x.

However, in this course, we are not actually interested in accuracy, and hence we don't need to make any predictions.

What we are interested in in this class is sampling.

So, there are two ways to sample from this model.

And by the way, this all might seem trivial, but these concepts will appear again later, so you'll want to make sure you keep them in mind.

So, the first way to draw a sample is to pick a class.

Let's say we want class y equals one.

Now, we know that p of x given y equals one is a Gaussian, and we know how to sample from Gaussians.

So, we can draw a sample from this Gaussian pretty easily using psi pi.

The second way to sample is just as easy mechanically, but is conceptually a little different.

You can picture our model as a graphical model.

Each node is a different distribution.

In particular, we have the distributions p of y and p of x given y.

This diagram makes the dependence on x and y very clear.

We can see that y has an influence on what x looks like.

And this makes sense.

If y equals 1, then the image we generate is going to look very different than if y equals 8.

But the key point is that y has its own distribution.

It's a categorical distribution or a discrete distribution.

To learn p of y, we just take the number of times each image has shown up in the training data and divide it by the number of training samples.

But because it is its own distribution, that means we can sample from it too.

So in other words, the first method of sampling we talked about is just sampling from a given Gaussian, which is just calling a function in psi pi.

Our sampling function is going to take in one parameter y so that we can decide which Gaussian to sample from.

The second method of sampling is conceptually different.

We are now sampling from the joint distribution p of x and y.

Since x depends on y, we first sample from the prior p of y, which we can learn from the data, and then we sample from p of x given y, which is what we already know how to do from earlier.

Now I think this is sufficient detail to implement something on your own.

So at this point, I'm assuming you've already downloaded the data and you know how to load it in using NumPy.

So the next exercise is going to be write a Bayes classifier that can fit to the data and generate samples from it using the two methods we just talked about.

Sampling from a given y, which is in other words p of x given y, and sampling from the joint distribution p of x and y.

6 - 采样演示：贝叶斯分类器

大家好，欢迎回到《无监督深度学习》第二部分。

这节课，我们要用代码实现一个贝叶斯分类器，并从我们的模型中生成一些样本。

如果你不想自己尝试写代码，尽管我强烈建议你这么做了，那课程仓库里的相关文件是BayesClassifier Gaussian.py。

我们首先在这里导入的是util。

注意，这指的是本地文件夹里的util.py。

它里面包含一些有用的功能，比如加载数据。

接下来，我们还导入了numpy、matplotlib，当然也少不了用来抽取样本的多元正态分布。

接着，我们定义了贝叶斯分类器这个类。

没有构造器，所以我们直接来到fit函数。

我们首先获取k，也就是类别的数量。

然后，我们初始化一个列表，用来存放我们的高斯分布信息。

请记住，描述一个高斯分布的两个参数，分别是它的均值和协方差。

所以，在一个循环里，我们为每个类别先找出所有属于该类的x，计算其均值，求出其协方差，然后把这些信息保存在一个名为g的字典里。

之后，我们把g字典追加到那个高斯列表里，以便后续使用。

拟合部分的全部内容就是这样。

请注意，因为我们预期类别编号是从零到k减一，所以默认情况下，每个类别刚好会索引到高斯列表中对应的那个高斯分布。

接下来，我们有一个从给定类别y抽取样本的函数。

这个函数所做的，就是找到跟这个类别对应的高斯分布，正如我们讨论过的，这仅仅是用索引在高斯列表里查找，然后我们会调用RVs函数来生成一个样本。

接下来，我们还有一个可从任意类别抽样一个样本的函数。

所以，它首先会随机选一个类别y，这里假定它们是均匀分布的。

严格来说，它们并非完全均匀分布，因为训练数据中每个类别的样本数是略有差异的。

但我们会忽略这个细节。

一旦选定了y，我们便直接调用前面定义好的“基于给定y采样”的那个函数了。

接下来是主程序部分。

在这里，我们拿到数据，创建一个贝叶斯分类器的实例，并让它拟合我们的数据。

然后，对于每一个类别，我们抽取一个样本，同时也展示该类别的均值图像。

我们把这两者并排绘制出来。

最后，我们抽取一个随机样本，这同时也意味着会随机选一个类别。

现在，让我们运行这段代码，看看结果如何。

请注意，所有东西看起来似乎都有些模糊。

这是我们在整个课程中都试图去解决的一个问题。

但与此同时，这些图像也并非和真实数字完全无法区分，这至少算是个不错的开端。

英文原文

Hey everyone and welcome back to this class, Unsupervised Deep Learning, Part 2.

In this lecture, we are going to implement a Bayes classifier in code and generate some samples from our model.

If you don't want to try to code this yourself, although I strongly recommend you do, the relevant file in the course repo is BayesClassifier Gaussian.py.

So the first thing we import here is util.

Note that this refers to util.py in the local folder.

This just contains useful stuff like loading in the data.

Next we have numpy matplotlib and of course the multivariate normal for drawing samples.

Next we define the Bayes classifier class.

No constructor so we go straight to the fit function.

So first we get k the number of classes.

Then we initialize a list to hold our Gaussian information.

Remember the two parameters that describe a Gaussian are its mean and covariance.

So inside a loop for each class we first get all the x's belonging to this class, find its mean, find its covariance, and then save this in a dictionary called g.

We then append g to our list of Gaussians for later use.

So that's everything for fitting.

Notice that because we expect the classes to be numbered from 0 to k minus 1, each class will index the appropriate Gaussian in our list of Gaussians by default.

Next we have a function for drawing a sample from a given class y.

All this does is finds the Gaussian corresponding to this class, which as we discussed involves only indexing our list of Gaussians, and then we will call the RVs function to generate a sample.

Next we have a function for drawing a sample from any class.

Actually they are not quite uniformly distributed since the number of samples per class in the training data is slightly different.

Once we've chosen a y, we can simply call the previous function we defined sample given y.

Next we have the main section.

7 - 高斯混合模型回顾

大家好，欢迎回到《无监督深度学习》第二部分。

这节课，我们要来扩展我们的贝叶斯分类器。

回想一下，我们之前用单一高斯分布的代码示例，得出了一个主要结论：生成的结果看起来非常模糊。

为什么会这样呢？

嗯，由于我们强制让每个给定 y 下 x 的分布 p 都是单一高斯分布，这就等于硬要拿一个单峰高斯分布去拟合一个多模态分布。

多模态是什么意思呢？

要记住，众数指的是一个随机变量最常见的那个值。

对于一个有概率密度函数的连续型随机变量来说，众数就是概率密度函数上的任何一个局部极大值。

当我们谈论多模态分布时，我们指的就是那种有多个峰值的分布。

这在谈论手写数字时就说得通了。

为什么这么说呢？

你看，并不是每个人都会用同一种方式来写同一个数字，但你可以想象，人们写一个数字的不同方式，总数是有限的。

所以举个例子，我们假设有一百个数字二的样本。

五百个人写出来的二，风格和左边的那种相似；另外五百个人写出来的二，风格则跟右边的那种相似。

如果我们试图把这些二建模成一个单一的分布，那我们就会说它有一个双模态分布。

重点在于，在整个数据集中，峰值的数量必然是有限的。

把一个二画得不像一个二之前，你能够采用的画法是有限的。

你画出来的二，不可能跟其他人的差异大到太离谱，因为到了那种地步，它看起来大概就不像本想写的那个数字了。

所以，我想我们都能认同，手写数字很可能就是一个多模态分布。

那么，在一个生成式贝叶斯分类器里，我们该如何为一个多模态分布建模呢？

这个嘛，我们以前见过用来做这件事的工具，具体来说，就是高斯混合模型。

如果你过去没上过聚类或高斯混合模型的课，也不用太担心，因为学习它们的工作原理，并不是本课程真正的重点所在。

我们会直接使用一个预构建好的 GMM。

关于 GMM，需要知道的重要一点是，它能以不同比例同时拟合多个高斯分布，以此来近似一个多模态分布。

那么，我们聊聊 GMM 的一些有趣事实。GMM 是一种隐变量模型，而且这一点对本课程来说很有意思。

无监督学习，讲的全都是隐变量。

在 GMM 里，我们称这个隐变量为 z，它代表了数据 x 到底属于哪个簇。

有趣的是，高斯混合模型的最终概率密度函数，看起来很像我们的贝叶斯分类器。

所以举个例子，假如一个模型有两个簇，那么 x 的分布 p 就等于 z 等于一的概率 p 乘以给定 z 等于一下 x 的分布 p，再加上 z 等于二的概率 p 乘以给定 z 等于二下 x 的分布 p。

我们可以把 z 的分布 p 看作是任何一个 x 属于某个特定簇的先验概率。

意思就是，还没看到任何 x 的时候，随便选一个点，它属于该簇的概率。

你看这个等式的时候，不应该觉得有多复杂，因为里面的每一部分我们都知道是什么。

z 的分布 p 同样是一个类别分布或说离散分布，在实现上，就是一个其和为壹的数值向量。

而每一个给定 z 下 x 的分布 p，又是一个单一的高斯分布。

所以，z 的分布 p 是一个先验，告诉我们在看到任何 x 之前，你大概率会属于哪个簇。

举个具体的例子，这就好比问了一千个人是否患有某种疾病，而患病人群所占的比例，就是那个 z 等于一的先验概率 p。

另外澄清一下，因为我以前见过有人犯这个错误，z 的分布 p，和给定 x 下 z 的分布 p，它俩不是一回事。

打个比方，z 的分布 p 就好比是在总人口中，患上这种疾病的人的频率。

但给定 x 下 z 的分布 p，就好比是一位病人去了诊所，医生做了一项检查，来判断这位病人到底有没有患上这种疾病。

这时，这个概率所基于的是来自病人、我们称之为 x 的数据，而不仅仅是人群中的比例，那个单纯的 z 的分布 p。

那么，假设我们拿到了一个 x，并且我们想知道它属于哪个簇。

嗯，这时我们便可以通过应用贝叶斯规则，来求出给定 x 下 z 的分布 p。

这很简单，因为等式右边的每一项我们都已经知道了。

给定 z 下 x 的分布 p，如前所述，就是一个单一高斯分布。

z 的分布 p 是先验，它只是一个类别分布，而 x 的分布 p 是证据项，就是我们前面展示过的那部分求和。

现在请记住，这是一门深度学习课。

我们现在提到这个隐变量 z 的原因，并不是我想教你 GMM 本身，这些我在另一门课里讲过了，而是因为它是个有用的概念，值得我们现在回顾一下，为以后做铺垫。

那么，这里还有一点非常有趣。

高斯混合模型是用一种叫做期望最大化，简称 EM 的算法来训练的。

我们在这门课里并不关心算法的细节，但重点在于，我们为什么需要用它，以及它在整个宏观体系中处在什么位置。

所以，当我们拥有一个隐变量模型，又无法用闭式解来求得参数的最大似然解时，我们就会用到 EM 算法。

但请记住，而且这是人们常常会搞混的一个重点，我们这里并没有什么特别的目标函数。

我们仍然就是想做最大似然估计。

举一个简单的例子，假如你想用高斯分布来为你机器学习课上学生的身高建模。

于是你测量了所有学生的身高，算出样本均值和样本标准差，这就得到了你的高斯分布。

如你所知，我们是有简单的公式来求样本均值和样本标准差的，但正是最大似然原理，让你能定义出这些公式。

具体来说，就是你要为似然函数找到一个表达式，然后关于参数，也就是均值和标准差，用微积分求导来最大化它。

我们对 EM 想表达的就是，当你尝试这样去解的时候，它没有解析解，但最终的目标还是一样的。

那么，为什么这很关键呢？

嗯，等我们开始讨论变分自编码器时，其中一个核心组件就是变分推断。

而变分推断就很像是 EM 的贝叶斯版本，所以理清这些概念是怎么串起来的，是件好事。

但变分推断之所以有趣，还有另外一个原因。

你可能还记得，像 k 均值聚类和高斯混合模型这类算法的一个弱点，就是你得事先选定簇的数量，作为算法的一个超参数。

而一旦选错了，你的模型表现就会很差。

那么，这里有个巧妙的技巧。

你可以使用高斯混合模型的变分推断版本，它实际上包含了无穷多个簇。

然而，这些簇中的大多数都会保持空置，因此，变分 GMM 在某种意义上，会自动帮你找到簇的数量。

至于它实际做得好不好，那又是另一个问题了，但我们暂且相信它能做到吧。

在这门课里，既然重点是深度学习，而不是贝叶斯机器学习，我们不会去做任何变分推断，也不会去手动实现 GMM，不过幸运的是，scikit-learn 自带了变分推断的 GMM，我们可以直接拿来用。

最后，我想向你介绍一些简单的图模型知识，帮我们用一种更贴合贝叶斯视角的方式来直观理解我们的模型。

实际上，这就是一个非常简单的贝叶斯网。

你可能还记得，在早些时候的贝叶斯分类器里，我们画过一张简单的图，用来表示 y 指向 x。

所以，在我们的新模型里，我们把给定 y 下 x 的分布 p 建模成一个 GMM，现在它引入了一个叫做 z 的隐变量。

因此，在图上，我们展示的是 y 指向 z，z 再指向 x。

就数据的生成方式或说样本的生成而言，你只需顺着箭头的方向走。

这是一个三步的过程。

首先，你对 y 采样，或者如果你想生成某个特定数字的图像，y 也可以是指定的。

然后，你为这个数字 y 采样一个簇 z。

这代表了人们书写某个特定数字时可能采用的不同方式。

再然后，我们采样给定 z 下的 x，这是一个单一的高斯分布，代表了一个数字的图像。

总结一下，这节课大部分都是在讲概念和思路，但实现起来应该是非常简单的。

具体来说，你要把你之前做的贝叶斯分类器拿出来，把里面的单一高斯分布，给替换成 scikit-learn 里的变分 GMM。

当然了，那个变分 GMM 自带 fit 和 sample 函数，所以代码上没什么太多要写的，不过你确实得遵循它的 API，也就是说，你得去检查一下返回类型都是些什么，这样才能正确地使用它们。

那么，先自己动手试一试吧，我们下一节课会看一些代码。

英文原文

Hey everyone and welcome back to this class, Unsupervised Deep Learning Part 2.

In this lecture, we are going to extend our Bayes classifier.

Recall that one of the main takeaways from our previous code example with a single Gaussian was that the results appeared very blurry.

Why did that happen?

Well, by forcing each p of x given y to be a single Gaussian, we had to force a single Gaussian to fit a multimodal distribution.

What do we mean by multimodal?

Remember that the mode is the most common value of a random variable.

In terms of a continuous random variable that has a PDF, it's any local maximum of the PDF.

When we're talking about multimodal distributions, we mean a distribution that has multiple peaks.

This makes sense when we're talking about digits.

Why is that?

Well, not everyone is going to write the same digit in the same way, but you can imagine there are a finite number of different ways people can write a digit.

So for example, we can have, say, 100 samples of twos.

500 people write twos similar to the style on the left, 500 people write two similar to the style on the right.

If we're trying to model these twos as one distribution, we would say that it has a bimodal distribution.

The important point is that the number of peaks in our full data set must be finite.

There are only so many ways you can draw a two before it ceases to look like a two.

You can't get so different from everyone else because then it probably wouldn't look like the intended digit at that point.

So I think we can agree that digits are probably a multimodal distribution.

So how can we model a multimodal distribution in a generative Bayes classifier?

Well, we've seen the tools to do this before, in particular, the Gaussian mixture model.

If you haven't taken a course on clustering or Gaussian mixture models in the past, don't worry too much because learning how they work is not really the focus of this course.

We will be using a pre-built GMM.

What is important to know about the GMM is that it fits multiple Gaussians simultaneously in different proportions to approximate a multimodal distribution.

So let's talk about some interesting facts concerning GMMs. The GMM is a latent variable model, and this particular fact is interesting for this course.

Unsupervised learning is all about latent variables.

With GMMs we call the latent variable z, and it represents which cluster the data x belongs to.

Interestingly, the final P d F of a Gaussian mixture model looks a lot like our Bayes classifier.

So for example, with a model that has two clusters, P of x is equal to p of z equals 1 times p of x given z equals 1 plus p of z equals 2 times p of x given z equals 2.

We can think of p of z as the prior probability that any x belongs to a certain cluster.

That means without looking at any x at all, that would be the probability that any randomly chosen point belonged to the cluster.

When you look at this equation, it shouldn't look too complicated because we know what each of these pieces is.

P of z is again a categorical or discrete distribution and implementation wise just a vector of numbers that sum to one.

Each p of x given z is again a single Gaussian.

So P of Z is a prior and tells us without looking at any x which cluster you're likely to belong to.

To give a concrete example, this would be like asking 1,000 people if they have some disease and the proportion of the people who have the disease would be the prior p of z equals 1.

And just to be clear, because I have seen people make this mistake before, p of z is not the same as p of z given x.

To give you an analogy, p of z would just be the frequency of people in the population who have the disease.

But p of z given x would be if a patient went to the doctor's office and the doctor performed a test to determine whether or not this patient had the disease.

Now the probability is based on data from the patient, which we call x, rather than just the population proportion, which is just p of z.

So let's say we are given an x, and we want to know which cluster it belongs to.

Well then we can find p of z given x by applying Bayes rule.

This is easy because we know each of the things on the right hand side already.

P of x given z is just a single Gaussian as we discussed earlier.

P of z is the prior, it's just a categorical distribution, and p of x is the evidence, it's the sum that we showed earlier.

Now keep in mind, this is a deep learning course.

The reason we are mentioning this latent variable z right now is not because I want to teach you about GMMs, which we've already done in another course, but it's because it's a useful concept to review for later.

So here's another thing that's really interesting.

The Gaussian mixture model is trained using an algorithm called Expectation Maximization, or EM for short.

We don't care about the details of the algorithm in this class, but the important point is why we need to use it and where it fits in the grand scheme of things.

So, we use EM when we have a latent variable model and we can't solve for the maximum likelihood solution of the parameters in closed form.

But keep in mind, and this is an important point where people get confused often, we don't have any special objective here.

We still just want to do maximum likelihood.

A simple example of this is when you want to model the heights of the students in your machine learning class as a Gaussian.

So you measure all your students, take the sample mean and sample standard deviation, and that's your Gaussian.

As you know, we have simple equations for finding the sample mean and sample standard deviation, but it's the principle of maximum likelihood that allows you to define these equations.

In particular, you find an expression for the likelihood and maximize it using calculus with respect to the parameters, the mean and standard deviation.

All we're saying with EM is when you try to do this, there is no solution, but the goal is still the same.

So why is this important?

Well, when we start discussing variational autoencoders, one key component is variational inference.

And variational inference is like a Bayesian version of EM, so it's nice to know how everything fits together.

But there is also one more reason why variational inference is interesting.

As you may recall, one weakness of algorithms like k-means clustering and Gaussian mixture models is that you need to choose the number of clusters as a hyperparameter into the algorithm.

And when you choose wrong, your model will be bad.

So here's a neat trick.

You can use the variational inference version of the Gaussian mixture model, which actually contains an infinite number of clusters.

However, most of those clusters will remain empty, and therefore the variational GMM will in some way automatically find the number of clusters for you.

Now whether it does this effectively or not is another question, but we will trust that it does.

In this course, since the focus is deep learning and not Bayesian machine learning, we won't be doing any variational inference or implementing GMMs, but luckily scikit-learn comes with a variational inference GMM that we can use.

Finally, I want to introduce you to some simple graphical modeling to help us visualize our model in a more Bayesian way.

In fact, this is a very simple Bayes net.

You may recall from our earlier Bayes classifier that we had a simple diagram where we showed y going to x.

So our new model, where we model p of x given y as a g m m, now includes a latent variable called z.

So in a picture, we show y going to z going to x.

In terms of how data is generated or samples, you just follow the arrows.

It's a three-step process.

First, you sample y, or it can be a given if you want to generate an image from a particular digit.

Then you sample one of the clusters z for this digit y.

This represents the different ways people can write a particular digit.

Then we sample x given z, which is a single Gaussian and represents an image of a digit.

To summarize, most of this lecture was just concepts and ideas, but the implementation should be very simple.

In particular, you're going to take our previous base classifier and switch out the single Gaussian with a variational GMM from scikit-learn.

And of course that already comes with a fit and sample function, so there isn't much work to do there, although you do have to conform to the API, which means you'll have to inspect the return types to see what they are so that you can use them appropriately.

So give this a try yourself first, and then in the next lecture, we will look at some code.

8 - 采样演示：基于高斯混合模型的贝叶斯分类器

大家好，欢迎回到《无监督深度学习》第二部分。

这节课，我们要用代码实现一个带有 GMM 的贝叶斯分类器，并从我们的模型中生成一些样本。

如果你不想自己尝试写代码，尽管我强烈建议你这么做了，那课程仓库里的相关文件是BayesClassifier gmm.py。

你会在这个脚本里注意到的第一件事是，它跟之前那个单一高斯版本的脚本几乎一模一样，除了我们现在会用到 scikit-learn 的变分高斯混合模型这一点。

那具体有哪些改变呢？

这个嘛，我们仍然有一个叫贝叶斯分类器的类，它里面也依然包含一个高斯列表。

但这里的每个高斯，会是一个贝叶斯高斯混合对象。

请注意它的构造器接收一个参数。

这个参数是什么呢？

嗯，如果你查阅一下文档，会发现它是一个整数，用来指定簇的最大数量。

所以，虽然变分 GMM 从理论上讲可以有无限多个簇，但现实中它总得有个上限，这个上限就充当了簇数量的硬性停止点。

我随意选了十。

可能有更多或更少的自然簇，但我没深入研究过。

在这里，我们也会在当前类别的数据上，调用 GMM 的 fit 函数。

请注意，这步操作会花相当长的时间，因为它需要执行变分推断的更新，这是一个迭代算法。

接下来，和之前一样，我们有一个用于从给定类别生成样本的函数。

这函数不同的地方在于，我们现在调用的是 GMM 的 sample 函数。

如果你去查一下，会发现这个函数会返回两个东西。

它在一个元组里，同时返回了样本本身，以及这个样本来自哪一个簇。

现在呢，因为我很好奇那个簇的均值长什么样，所以我把这个均值也取了出来，并作为另一张图像返回。

这样，我们就能像在上一个脚本里做的那样，把样本和高斯均值都绘制出来，只不过现在这个高斯均值，只是每个类别下众多可能均值中的一种。

请注意，我在这里用到的一些东西，并不在 scikit-learn 的官方文档里。

你得去翻看他们放在 GitHub 上的源代码，才能找出你能查看哪些属性。

接下来是主程序部分，这跟之前几乎一样，就是为每个类别打印一个样本，然后再以随机选中的类别生成一个样本。

那么，让我们运行一下看看结果如何。

哦，也别忘了，要准备好比跑上一个脚本等上久得多的时间。

那么，关于这些图像，你能看到的一点是，它们看起来比单一高斯版本的要稍微逼真那么一丁点，模糊感也稍弱一些。

这当然是因为单一高斯试图一次性去近似所有的书写风格，而 GMM 则能针对每种不同的风格，有其各自独立的高斯分布。

英文原文

Hey everyone and welcome back to this class, Unsupervised Deep Learning, Part 2.

In this lecture, we are going to implement a Bayes classifier with a GMM in code and generate some samples from our model.

If you don't want to try to code this yourself, although I strongly recommend you do, the relevant file in the course repo is BayesClassifier gmm.py.

So the first thing you'll notice in this script is that it's almost identical to the previous single Gaussian script, except for the fact that we'll now be using scikit-learn's variational Gaussian mixture model.

So what's changed?

Well, we still have a class called Bayes classifier, and it still contains a list of Gaussians.

But each Gaussian is going to be a Bayesian Gaussian mixture object.

Notice how its constructor takes in one argument.

What is this argument?

Well, if you check the documentation, it's an integer that specifies the maximum number of clusters.

So while variational GMMs technically have an infinite number of clusters, in reality it has to stop at some point, and so this upper limit acts as a hard stop for the number of clusters.

I've chosen 10 arbitrarily.

There could be more or less natural clusters, but I haven't looked into it.

In here, we also call the fit function on the GMM for the data in the current class.

Notice that this will take quite long since it needs to perform the variational inference update, which is an iterative algorithm.

Next, we have a function for generating a sample from a given class as before.

What's different about this is we're now calling the GMM sample function.

This will return two things if you check.

It returns both the sample itself and which cluster it came from in a tuple.

Now, since I am curious what the mean of the cluster looks like, I'm grabbing that and returning it as an image too.

So this will allow us to plot both a sample and a Gaussian mean like we did in the previous script, except now this Gaussian mean is just one of many possible means per class.

Note that some of the stuff I'm using here isn't in the official scikit-learn documentation.

You'd have to look through their code which is on GitHub to find out which attributes you can look at.

Next we have the main section, which is pretty much the same as before, just printing a sample for each class and then one sample with a randomly selected class.

So let's run this and see what we get.

Oh, and don't forget to be prepared to wait much longer than the previous script.

So one thing you can see about these images is that they seem to be a tiny bit more realistic and less blurry than the single Gaussian versions.

This is of course because a single Gaussian is trying to approximate all styles at once, whereas a GMM can have distinct Gaussians for each of the different styles.

9 - 为什么需要生成样本

大家好，欢迎回到《无监督深度学习》第二部分。

这一讲，我们要来回答一个问题：我们到底为什么要在意生成式建模？

这是个很合理的问题。

很多刚接触机器学习的人，他们并不是在研究机器学习本身，而是想立刻得到这类典型问题的答案。

我怎么用机器学习预测股票来赚钱？

我怎么为自动驾驶汽车写软件？

这些当然都是很好的问题，但在研究机器学习的过程中，它们并不是正确的问题。

这就像你去了健身房，却跟教练说，教我举起那五百磅的杠铃。

教练会说，嗯，你得先学会举起一百磅的，再举二百磅的、三百磅的，然后是四百磅的。

之后你才能举起那五百磅的。

很多人不爱听这话。

他们现在就想举起那五百磅的。

忘了那四百磅的吧。

忘了合理的饮食，忘了要风雨无阻地坚持锻炼一整年。

他们今天就想举起那五百磅。

然而，我想你从这么直白的比方里也能看出来，这些点点滴滴，恰恰都是在帮助你、支撑你走向举起五百磅的最终目标。

事实上，如果你不做好这些，要想举起那五百磅，十有八九是不可能的。

同样的，如果你想成为一名机器学习大师，并且真正懂得如何将它应用于自动驾驶汽车和其他这类现代科技，那你就需要去探索机器学习中监督学习之外的其他维度。

这并非巧合，这个领域的专家，像 Lacun、Benjio、Hinton 等等，都把大量注意力放在了无监督学习上。

正如我们之前所见，无监督学习帮助我们构建了一套强大的理论工具，让你在监督学习上也能做得更好，但这还不是全部，有些无监督学习的任务还能直接辅助并改善监督学习。

我们恰恰就看到了这点，用自编码器和受限玻尔兹曼机对监督神经网络进行逐层贪婪预训练。

与此同时，生成式建模的作用就没那么直接。

我们有些想法，但从构建一个基础生成式模型，到真正将其用于这些实际应用之间，还存在巨大的鸿沟。

很多这类应用还处在研究阶段。

人们还在不断探索。

不过，它们很令人激动，并且拥有切实的可能性，所以值得我们去探讨。

那么，我们先设定一个前提：不论我们的自编码器或生成式模型有多好，它生成的样本，还是比不上原始数据。

那么，我们为什么要生成那些不如我们手中已有图片的图像呢？

不但如此，与贝叶斯分类器不同，GAN 默认甚至都不去学习那个分布。

所以，按照这个标准，连我们已经写出来的那个简单贝叶斯分类器都显得更有用，因为我们从中得到了数据概率密度函数的直接近似。

那么，应用到底有哪些呢？

其中一个应用是强化学习。

在强化学习里，一个智能体必须通过体验其环境来学习。

那么，我们举个简化的例子，假设我们在打造一辆自动驾驶汽车。

在平坦无阻的道路上开车，这很简单。

驾驶的难点在于路上有障碍：行人、车流、坑洼、不平的地形、天气等等。

尤其是在这些危险状况下，你才格外需要确信你的自动驾驶应用懂得如何正确应对。

同时，这样一个应用又需要从真实世界的经验中学习。

那么，难道我们为了要让程序学会所有这些可能性，就真的去撞一百万次车吗？

这似乎不太可能。

那我们要如何确保自己的自动驾驶应用，知道在危险情况下该怎么做呢？

嗯，一个答案是，我们可以模拟这种经历。

总的来说，如果我们能够模拟环境，让智能体从中学习，那我们就能避免使用真实环境，而如你所见，用真实环境的代价可能非常大。

另一个应用是半监督学习。

你可能到现在已经了解到，深度学习通常在拥有大量有标签数据可供训练时，效果才最好。

当然，这既昂贵又耗时。

想象一下，你独自在台式电脑上，翻阅一百万张图片，去一个个弄清它们该对应什么标签。

同时，我们又喜欢监督学习，喜欢有标签。

它给了我们一个相对直接的任务去执行。

而且，有时候我们并不想做纯粹的无监督学习。

所以，一个值得思考的问题是，是否有可能将带标签和无标签的数据以某种方式结合起来，做半监督学习。

另一种应用场景是处理多模态分布。

我们已经看到，通过将手写数字图像建模为多模态高斯分布，我们能极大改善生成效果。

神经网络同样有能力对多模态分布建模，而如你将看到的，GAN 尤其擅长这一点。

所以，其思路是，当你使用一个典型的误差函数，比如平方误差时，神经网络会试图同时瞄准所有真实目标的中心，其结果就是一张模糊的图像。

那么，在你看到的这张图里，我们就能看到这个结果。

任务是尝试预测视频的下一帧。

左边的图是真实标准值。

中间的图是你用平方误差做代价函数时得到的结果。

右边的图则是你用对抗训练得到的结果。

另一个应用场景，你可能在电影里见过。

一个常见的情节是，你拍到了某张模糊的图像，然后想用算法来增强它。

这个嘛，我们有算法来做这件事已经很久了，但它们并不都能产出高质量、逼真的图像。

这也被称为上采样，换句话说，就是让一张小图像变得比原先更大。

另一个应用，是我在课程导论里谈到过的：创造艺术。

科学通常被视为艺术的对立面，但也许未来的艺术将由AI而非人类创造。

说不定它甚至比人类能创造出来的还要好。

至少，AI在创造这些事物的速度与效率上，会比人类高出不少。

想象一下，未来排行榜上前四十的热门歌曲，其演唱者甚至不是人类。

这很真实，没有哪个职业能免于AI的冲击。

还有一个应用，叫做图像到图像的翻译。

我们能取一张信息量较低的图像，比如一幅草图，然后将其转变成一张看起来非常逼真的图像。

在这段视频里，我只展示了一个很短的片段，但你可以在课程代码仓库里找到完整视频的链接。

你可以看到，简单的草图是怎么被转变成完整且逼真的风景画的。

有意思的是，这同样可以用于创意行业，比如设计服装或配饰。

想象一下，仅仅画个草图，然后让计算机来填充所有的颜色、材质和其他细节。

这是来自我自己另一门课中的一个例子。

所以，目标是生成一些看起来真实的东西。

在我的NLP课上，我们做了一个关于文章伪原创的项目。

文章伪原创，以及广义上的文章自动写作，这可以说是该领域的圣杯之一。

如果内容能自动生成，那将永久性地改变许多行业。

想一想，你现在之所以在学这门课，是因为它是由一个能教给你东西的人类设计的。

你可能每天都会浏览固定的那两三个网站来看新闻。

如果你正在吸收的这些信息，实际上全都来自一个机器人，会不会感觉很奇怪？

如果这门课是一个机器人做的呢？

要是一台机器不仅能吸引你的注意力，甚至还可能说服你去花钱，或是跟朋友们分享些什么，那会怎样？

要是电视节目、电影、杂志等等，全都是由机器人生成的，并且还根据你的个性精准投放，让你上瘾，然后花费更多的时间和金钱去追逐更多，那又会是怎样一番景象？

那可真就是市场营销人员的梦想了。

但不管怎样，让我们回到文章伪原创器上来。

正如一些学生敏锐指出的那样，用马尔可夫链伪原创生成的文章，质量并不怎么样。

当然，在那个项目里，理解原理才是目标，这就跟我们之前在贝叶斯分类器里采样数字图像一样。

显然，这类简单方法在生成质量上，都还有很多不尽如人意之处，因此，能够生成逼真文本和图像样本的能力，是极其有价值的。

而这，正是变分自编码器，特别是GAN，所能带给我们的。

不是说我们以前就没有生成式建模。

我们一直都能生成样本，但那些样本的质量却未必好。

让GAN有趣的地方在于，它生成的样本质量极其出色，而在理论层面，它采用了一种叫做对抗训练的新颖训练技术，这在之前是闻所未闻的。

所以，请你抱有兴趣，因为深度学习本身就很有意思，而且它能产生很酷的结果；不要再觉得，凡是没法解决你股票预测问题的东西，就都是没用的了。

英文原文

Hey everyone and welcome back to this class, Unsupervised Deep Learning, Part 2.

In this lecture, we are going to answer the question, why do we care about generative modeling in the first place?

This is a legitimate question.

A lot of newcomers to machine learning, rather than studying machine learning itself, they want immediate answers to these typical questions.

How can I use machine learning to predict stocks and make money?

How can I write software for a self-driving car?

Now these are fine questions, but they are not the right questions in the study of machine learning.

This is like going to the gym and asking the trainer, teach me how to lift the 500 pound weight.

The trainer says, well, first you have to learn how to lift the 100 pound weight, the 200 pound weight, the 300 pound weight, and the 400 pound weight.

Then you can lift the 500 pound weight.

A lot of people don't like hearing that.

They want to lift the 500 pound weight right now.

Forget the 400 pound weight.

Forget the proper diet, forget exercising every day for a year.

They want to lift the 500 pound weight today.

However, and I think you can discern this from such an explicit analogy, these things all help and support you on your path to lifting the 500 pound weight.

In fact, if you don't do these things, getting to the 500 pound weight will most likely be impossible.

Similarly, if you want to become a machine learning master and really know how to apply it to self-driving cars and other such modern technologies, then you'll want to explore other facets of machine learning other than just supervised learning.

It's not a coincidence that the experts in this field, Lacun, Benjio, Hinton, and more, have focused a lot of attention on unsupervised learning.

As we've seen before, unsupervised learning helps us develop a strong theoretical tool set that makes you better at supervised learning, but not only that, some unsupervised learning tasks directly support supervised learning and improve it.

We saw exactly that by using autoencoders and RBMs for greedy layer-wise pre-training of a supervised neural net.

At the same time, generative modeling is not so direct.

We have some ideas, but there's a big gap between building a basic generative model and actually using one for some of these applications.

A lot of these applications are still in the research stage.

People are still working on them.

However, they are exciting and they are real possibilities, so they are worth discussing.

So let's start with the premise that, no matter how good our autoencoder or our generative model is, the samples it creates are still not going to be as good as the originals.

So, why would we want to generate pictures that aren't as good as the pictures we already have?

In addition, unlike the Bayes classifier, the GAN by default doesn't even learn the distribution.

So, by that measure, even the simple Bayes classifier we already wrote is more useful because we get a direct PDF approximation of the data.

So, what are the applications?

One application is reinforcement learning.

In reinforcement learning, an agent has to learn by experiencing its environment.

So, let's say, as a simplified example, we are building a self-driving car.

Now, driving a car on a flat road with no obstacles is pretty simple.

Where driving becomes hard is when there are obstacles in the way: pedestrians, traffic, potholes, uneven terrain, weather, and so on.

It's especially in these dangerous situations that you want to be confident your self-driving application knows how to behave correctly.

At the same time, such an application needs to learn from real-life experience.

So, are we going to crash a car one million times in order for our program to learn all these possibilities?

That seems unlikely.

So, how can we ensure our self-driving application knows what to do in a dangerous situation?

Well, one answer is we could simulate such an experience.

In general, if we could simulate the environment such that an agent can learn from it, then we can avoid having to use the real environment, which, as you have seen, can be costly.

Another application is semi-supervised learning.

As you may have learned by now, deep learning typically works best when it has large amounts of labeled data to train from.

Of course, this is expensive and time-consuming.

Imagine clicking through one million pictures on your desktop computer by yourself and figuring out which labels apply to them.

At the same time, we like supervised learning and we like having labels.

It gives us a relatively straightforward task to perform.

And sometimes we don't want to do pure unsupervised learning.

So, a question worth considering is if it's possible to use both the labeled data and the unlabeled data in some combination to do semi-supervised learning.

Another application is working with multimodal distributions.

we've already seen that we can greatly benefit the generation of images of digits by modelling them as multimodal Gaussians.

Neural networks also have the ability to model multimodal distributions, and GANs are particularly good at doing so, as you will see.

So, the idea is that when you use a typical error function like the squared error, the neural network tries to pick the center of all the actual targets simultaneously, and the result of that is a blurred image.

So, in the image you see here, we can see the results of this.

The task is trying to predict the next frame in the video.

The left image is the ground truth.

The middle image is what you get if you use squared error as your cause function.

The image on the right is what you get when you use adversarial training.

Another application is something you've probably seen in the movies.

A common scenario is you'll have captured some blurry image, and you want to enhance it algorithmically.

Well, we've had algorithms to do this for a long time, but they do not all produce high-quality, realistic images.

This is also known as upsampling, or in other words, making a small image bigger than the original.

Another application is something I talked about in the introduction to this course: creating art.

Science is typically considered the opposite of art, but maybe the art of the future will be produced by AI rather than humans.

Maybe it will be even better than what humans can create.

At the very least, AI would be able to create these things faster and more efficiently than a human could.

Imagine top 40 music where the artist is not even human.

It's true, no profession is safe from AI.

Another application is called image-to-image translation.

We can take a low-information image like a sketch and turn it into a realistic-looking image.

In this video, I'm showing just a short clip here, but you can find the link to the full video in the course repo.

You can see how simple sketches can be turned into a full realistic landscape.

What's interesting about this is that this can also be used in creative professions, for example, designing clothes or accessories.

Imagine just drawing a sketch and having the computer fill in all the colors, textures, and other details.

Here's another example from one of my own courses.

So the goal is to generate something that seems realistic.

In my NLP class we do a project on article spinning.

Article spinning and in general article writing is something of a holy grail.

It would change industries forever if content could be automatically written.

Think about it, you are taking this course because it was designed by a human being that can teach you something.

You probably browse the same two or three websites for news every day.

Would it not be strange if all the information you're digesting actually came from a robot?

What if a robot made this course?

What if a machine could capture your attention, and not only that, but maybe convince you to spend money or share something with your friends?

What if TV shows, movies, magazines, and so on were all generated by robots and specifically targeted to your personality so that you'd become addicted to it and spend time and money trying to acquire more?

That would be a marketer's dream.

But anyway, back to our article spinner.

As some students have astutely noted, the articles that are spun by a Markov article spinner are not that good.

Of course, it's understanding the principles that's the goal in that case, just like sampling images of digits in our Bayes classifier.

Clearly, such simple methods leave a lot to be desired in terms of quality, and so being able to generate samples of believable text and images is extremely valuable.

And so that's what variational autoencoders and especially GANs give us.

It's not that we've never had generative modeling before.

We've always been able to generate samples, but the quality of those samples is not necessarily good.

What makes GANs interesting is that the quality of the samples it produces are exceptionally good, and on the theoretical side, they use a novel training technique called adversarial training that had never been considered before.

So be interested because deep learning is interesting and because it produces cool results, and stop thinking that anything that doesn't solve your stock prediction problem is not useful.

10 - 神经网络与自编码器回顾

大家好，欢迎回到《无监督深度学习》第二部分。

在这节课里，我们将简要回顾一下神经网络，特别是自编码器，为接下来的 Theano 和 TensorFlow 热身练习做准备。

提醒一下，这只是个回顾。

内容不会很深入，也不会有任何例子，所以如果你还没理解这里讨论的内容，请务必在继续之前把它弄懂。

回想一下，神经网络是一个非线性的函数逼近器。

这听起来可能很复杂，但其实不然。

只要你会做线性回归和逻辑回归，你就能搞定神经网络。

回想下，逻辑回归不过是对某个输入向量 x 做一次线性变换，输出的是一个概率。

如果我们在做二分类，那么输出的概率就是给定 x 下 y 等于一的概率 p，即我们认为 x 属于类别一而非类别零的概率。

那么，神经网络就是多层逻辑回归的堆叠。

你在这里看到的这些方程式，便是一个有 L 层的神经网络。

你可以把每一行想象成一个独立的逻辑回归。

在这个场景中，f 可以是任何非线性激活函数，比如 sigmoid、tanh、relu 等等。

如今一个相当典型的默认选择是 relu。

在输出层，我们会根据要解决的问题类型，使用一个不同的函数 g。

对于回归问题，g 是恒等函数，所以最后一层就像是一个线性回归层。

对于二分类问题，g 是 sigmoid 函数，这也是我们在这门课里会频繁用到的。

对于多分类问题，g 则是 softmax，这门课里我们多半不会用到它。

现在，神经网络通常用于监督学习，也就是分类或回归。

在这种情况下，我们需要实现两个主要函数：fit 和 predict。

预测，意味着从 x 推导出 y。

给定一个 x，我预测的 y 是什么？

拟合，则意味着调整权重，使得预测结果能接近训练数据中的目标值。

既然我刚刚给了你相关的方程式，我们当然知道如何做预测。

而在神经网络里，大部分的工作都花在拟合上。

我们用于拟合的主要工具，叫做梯度下降。

为了让梯度下降起作用，我们要构造一个代价函数，暂且称之为 J，然后让模型的参数沿着 J 的梯度方向移动。

这很合理，因为梯度告诉我们的正是最陡峭下降的方向。

你可以把 J 想象成一只碗，那我们的目标就是到达碗的底部。

你会看到，梯度下降是你将在本课程、乃至整个深度学习领域要学的所有算法里的一个关键组成部分。

使用神经网络的一个非常简单的方法是调用 scikit-learn 库。

它有常用的 API，即用 fit(X, y) 做训练，用 predict(X) 做预测。

有意思的是，我记得有位 scikit-learn 的开发者曾反对将神经网络纳入该库，因为它们并非像随机森林那样，是种即插即用的模型。

我以前也常被问到这个问题，主要是初学者在问。

他们会问，你为什么就不能直接给我们一个 API 呢？

我们为什么非得学导数啊？

嗯，最直接的回答是，要真正有效地使用深度学习，你没办法即插即用。

你必须基于神经网络的运作原理，做出明智的决策。

我觉得有趣的是这一点。

我们现在终于能在 scikit-learn 里看到一个针对神经网络的 API 了。

但假设你要搭建一个变分自编码器，或是一个 GAN。

你当然还是没法用 scikit-learn 这个库来做这些。

这门课讲的是用于无监督学习的神经网络，而我们可以搭建的最简单的无监督神经网络之一，就叫做自编码器。

那什么是自编码器呢？

自编码器中，“自”这个字是关键，它指的是“自身”。

换句话说，它是一个试图预测其自身输入的神经网络。

因此，我们不再用标准的 fit(X, y)，而是直接调用 fit(X, X)。

这个想法非常简洁。

自编码器最简单的架构是单隐藏层的神经网络。

你可以使用任何激活函数，但我在接下来的代码里会选用 relu。

自编码器的一个重要特征是，其隐藏层的大小通常会小于输入层的大小。

这迫使神经网络去学习一个高效、紧凑的输入表示。

换言之，它在做降维或压缩，也就是将输入数据编码成另一种格式，这便是“自编码器”这个名称的由来。

另一方面，一个有趣的事实是，搭建隐藏层大小超过输入大小的自编码器，仍然能够带来理想的结果。

有人可能会想当然地认为，如果你有 n 个训练样本和 n 个隐藏单元，神经网络可能就会简单地学会去复制它的训练数据，从而导致严重的过拟合。

然而，研究表明，这种情况并不会发生。

那么，一个明显的问题就是，我们为什么要这么做？

一个只会复现自己输入的神经网络，到底有什么用？

嗯，就如你在这门课后面会看到的，这让我们能够构建可以从中采样的生成式模型，而我们之前也已经讨论过一些生成样本的应用。

自编码器的另一个应用是无监督预训练，它已被证明能对监督训练带来好处。

我们在这门课的前作中讲解过这部分内容。

不过，它的运作方式跟本课程无关，你也不需要对其有所了解。

这只是个例子，说明一个单纯学会预测自身输入的神经网络，可能有什么样的用途。

而且，这些用途有时并不那么显而易见。

在接下来的几节课里，我们会分别用 Theano 和 TensorFlow 来实现我们刚刚讨论过的自编码器。

因此，为本课程的后续内容做准备，你最好亲自尝试一下写写看，并且要确保你对写这样的代码已经非常得心应手，因为本课程后续的所有代码，都依赖于你首先把这一步弄明白。

英文原文

Hey everyone and welcome back to this class, Unsupervised Deep Learning, Part 2.

In this lecture, we are going to do a brief review of neural networks and in particular autoencoders in preparation for our Theano and TensorFlow warm-up exercises.

Note that this is just a review.

It's not extensive and there aren't going to be any examples, so if you don't understand what's being discussed here yet, please make sure you do before moving on.

Recall that a neural network is a nonlinear function approximator.

That might sound complicated, but it's not.

If you can do linear regression and logistic regression, you can do neural networks.

Recall that logistic regression is just a linear transformation on some input vector x, and the output is a probability.

If we're doing binary classification, then the output probability is p of y equals 1 given x, the probability that we believe x belongs to the 1 class as opposed to the 0 class.

So a neural network is just multiple stacks of logistic regressions.

These equations you see here would be an L layer neural network.

You can think of each line as a separate logistic regression.

In this scenario, f can be any nonlinear activation function, like sigmoid, tanh, rel u, and so on.

A pretty typical default choice these days is rel u.

At the output, we have a different function g, depending on what type of problem we're trying to solve.

For regression problems, g is identity, so the last layer is like a linear regression layer.

For binary classification problems, g is a sigmoid, which is what we'll be using frequently in this class.

For multi-class classification problems, g is the SOFMAX, which we most likely won't be using in this class.

Now neural networks are typically used for supervised learning, which means classification or regression.

There are two main functions we have to implement in this case, fit and predict.

Predicting means going from x to y.

Given an x, what is my prediction y?

Fitting means adjusting the weights so that the predictions are close to the targets in the training data.

We know how to predict since I just gave you the equations for that.

Fitting is where most of the work is done in neural networks.

The main tool for fitting we have is called gradient descent.

In order for gradient descent to work, we build a cost function, let's call it j, and move the parameters of the model in the direction of the gradient of j.

This makes sense because the gradient tells us the direction of steepest descent.

If you imagine J like a bowl, our goal is to get to the bottom of the bull.

As you'll see, gradient descent is a crucial component to all the algorithms you'll learn about in this course and for deep learning in general.

One really easy way to use a neural network is to use the scikit-learn library.

It has the usual API, fit XY for training and predictX for prediction.

What's funny is, I recall a scikit-learn developer objecting to the inclusion of neural networks in the library because of the fact that they are not really a plug-and-play model like random forests.

And I used to get this question a lot too, from beginners mostly.

They would ask, why can't you just give us an API?

Why do we have to learn derivatives?

Well, the easy answer to this is that to actually use deep learning effectively, you can't just plug and play.

You have to make intelligent decisions based on how neural networks work.

What I find amusing is this.

Now we finally do have an API in scikit-learn for neural networks.

But let's say you want to build a variational autoencoder or a GAN.

And of course, you can't use the scikit-learn library for this anyway.

Now this course is about neural networks for unsupervised learning, and one of the simplest unsupervised neural networks we can build is called an autoencoder.

So what is an autoencoder?

The key term in autoencoder is auto, which means self.

In other words, it's a neural network that just predicts its own input.

So instead of the standard fit xy, we just call fit xx.

Pretty simple idea.

The simplest architecture for an autoencoder is a one-hidden layer neural network.

You can use any activation function, but I'll be using rel u in the upcoming code.

One important feature of autoencoders is that the hidden layer size is typically smaller than the input size.

This forces the neural network to learn an efficient and compact representation of the input.

In other words, it does dimensionality reduction or compression, or in other words, it encodes the input data into a different format, hence the name autoencoder.

On the other hand, one interesting fact is that building autoencoders with a hidden layer size bigger than the input size still leads to desirable outcomes.

One might assume that if you have n training samples and n hidden layers, the neural network might simply learn to copy its training data, which would lead to severe overfitting.

However, research has shown that this does not happen.

Now an obvious question is, why do we want to do this?

What use is a neural network that just reproduces its input?

Well, as you'll see later in this course, this allows us to build generative models that we can sample from, and we already discussed previously some of the applications of generating samples.

Another application of autoencoders is unsupervised pre-training, which has been shown to benefit supervised training.

We covered this in the prequel to this course.

However, the way this works is not relevant to this course, and you don't need to be familiar with it.

It's just an example of what uses one might have for a neural network that simply learns to predict its own input.

And sometimes those uses are not obvious.

In the next couple of lectures, we are going to go through a Fiano and TensorFlow implementation of the autoencoder we just discussed.

So, in preparation for the rest of the course, it would be a good idea to try this yourself, and also to make sure you're extremely comfortable writing this code, since all of the rest of the code in this course is dependent on you understanding this first.

11 - TensorFlow热身

大家好，欢迎回到《无监督深度学习》第二部分。

这节课，我们要用 TensorFlow 实现一个自编码器，以此作为编程的热身练习。

如果你不想自己尝试写代码，尽管我强烈建议你这么做了，那课程仓库里的相关文件是 autoencoder tf.py。

在脚本最上面，是我们所有常见的导入，包括 numpy、matplotlib、tensorflow，以及用于获取数据的 util。

接下来，我们定义了一个自编码器类，它将是一个单隐藏层的神经网络，使用 relu 激活函数，并且因为图像的像素值只介于零到一之间，输出层将采用 sigmoid 函数。

这份代码的大部分工作都在构造器里完成，所以你要重点看一下这里，才能了解这个文件的相关细节。

一开始，我们为输入数据 x 创建了一个占位符。

这会是一个 n 乘 d 的二维矩阵，但由于 n 可以变化，所以我们将其设为 none。

下一步，我们定义从输入到隐藏层的权重和偏置项。

这些权重的维度是 d 乘 m，其中 d 是输入的维度，m 是隐藏层的维度。

接着，我们再定义从隐藏层到输出层的权重和偏置项。

这些权重的维度正好相反，它们会把你从 m 维带回到 d 维，以便能重建输入。

下一步，我们通过创建一个关于输出的表达式，把所有这些变量串接起来。

请注意，我既需要 logits 值，也需要重建值，也就是 logits 经过 sigmoid 处理后的结果。

这是因为我所使用的代价函数，而这也是脚本接下来要讲的部分。

就代价函数而言，你可以用平方误差，并假定你的输入和输出服从高斯分布，或者也可以用交叉熵，就像我们在之前那门无监督深度学习课里做的那样，并假定你的输入和输出是伯努利概率。

在后一种情况下，TensorFlow 已经内置了交叉熵代价函数，但它接收的是 logits 值，这也就是我们上一步要显式把它们算出来的原因。

接下来，我们创建一个优化器，并告诉 TensorFlow 它要最小化的，就是我们刚刚定义的那个代价。

最后，我们定义一个会话，并将上面创建的变量全都初始化。

然后，我们定义一个 fit 函数。

它接收 x，也就是训练数据、epochs 的数量以及批处理大小。

在这个函数内部，我们通过循环来遍历所有 epochs 和 batches，在每个 epoch 中都会打乱数据，并在每个 batch 上调用我们之前定义好的训练更新操作。

我们会把代价都存下来，这样结束后就能绘制出相应的曲线。

这个函数之所以这么短，是因为大部分工作我们都在构造器里做完了。

接下来是 predict 函数，它的功能就是给定 x，计算出 x 的重建值 x_hat。

然后就是主函数了。

它会加载数据，创建一个隐藏层大小为三百的自编码器，将数据喂给它进行拟合，然后在一个循环里，不断随机生成重建图像。

这里的设计是，你可以敲击字母 n 键退出，或者按回车或其他任意键来继续生成随机的重建结果。

那么，让我们运行一下看看结果如何。

英文原文

Hey everyone and welcome back to this class, Unsupervised Deep Learning Part 2.

In this lecture, we are going to implement an autoencoder in TensorFlow as a coding warm-up.

If you don't want to try to code this yourself, although I strongly recommend you do, the relevant file in the course repo is autoencoder tf.py.

At the top, we have all our usual imports, numpy, matplotlib, tensorflow, and util for getting the data.

Next, we define our autoencoder class, which will be a one-hidden layer neural network with a relu activation function and sigmoid output, since images only have values between 0 and 1.

Most of the work in this code is in the constructor, so you want to look here for the relevant details of this file.

We start out by creating a placeholder for the input data x.

This is going to be a 2D matrix of size n by d, but n can be variable, so we leave it as none.

Next, we define the input to hidden weights and bias.

These weights are d by m, where d is the input dimensionality and m is the hidden layer dimensionality.

Next, we define the hidden to output weights and bias.

These weights are of the opposite shape, so they take you from m back to d in order to reconstruct the input.

Next, we join all these variables together by creating an expression for the output.

Notice how I want the logits and the reconstruction, which is the sigmoid of the logits.

This is because the cause function I'm using, which happens to be the next part of this script.

So for the cost function, you could use squared error and consider your inputs and outputs to be Gaussian, or you could use cross-entropy like we did in the previous unsupervised deep learning course and assume your inputs and outputs to be Bernoulli probabilities.

In that case, TensorFlow already has a cross-entropy cost function, but it takes in the logits, which is why we calculated them explicitly in the previous step.

Next, we create an optimizer and tell TensorFlow that it's going to minimize the cost that we just defined.

Finally, we define a session and init the variables from above.

Next, we define a fit function.

This takes in x, which is the training data, number of epochs, and batch size.

Inside this function, we loop through the number of epochs and the number of batches, shuffle the data over each epoch, and call the train-up we defined earlier on each batch.

We save the costs so we can plot them when we're done.

This function is short because we did most of the work in the constructor.

Next we have the predict function, which just calculates x hat given x.

Next we have the main function.

This loads in the data, creates an autoencoder with hidden layer size 300, fits it to the data, and then generates random reconstructions in a loop.

I've designed this so you can hit the letter n to quit, or just hit enter or any other character to continue generating random reconstructions.

So let's run this and see what we get.

12 - Theano热身

大家好，欢迎回到《无监督深度学习》第二部分。

这节课，我们要用 Theano 实现一个自编码器，以此作为编程的热身练习。

如果你不想自己尝试写代码，尽管我强烈建议你这么做了，那课程仓库里的相关文件是 autoencodertheano.py。

在脚本最上面，是我们所有常见的导入，包括 numpy、matplotlib、theano，以及用于获取数据的 util。

接下来，我们定义了一个自编码器类，它将是一个单隐藏层的神经网络，使用 relu 激活函数，并且因为图像的像素值只介于零到一之间，输出层将采用 sigmoid 函数。

大部分工作都在构造器里完成，所以你要重点看一下这里，才能了解这个文件的相关细节。

那么，我们一开始先为输入数据 x 创建了一个占位符。

这会是一个 n 乘 d 的二维矩阵，但我们不需要指定确切的形状。

下一步，我们定义从输入到隐藏层的权重和偏置项。

这些权重的维度是 d 乘 m，其中 d 是输入的维度，m 是隐藏层的维度。

接着，我们再定义从隐藏层到输出层的权重和偏置项。

这些权重的维度正好相反，它们会把你从 m 维带回到 d 维，以便能重建输入。

下一步，我们通过为输出 x_hat 创建一个表达式，把所有这些变量串接起来。

就代价函数而言，你可以用平方误差，并视输入与输出间的差异服从高斯分布，或者也可以用交叉熵，就像我们在之前那门无监督深度学习课里做的那样，并假定你的输入和输出是伯努利概率。

我们现在用的是后者，但你也尽管去试试平方误差。

接下来，我们设定训练流程。

第一步是把我们所有的参数放到一个列表里。

然后，我们可以求代价函数对所有这些参数的梯度。下一步，我们为 RMSProp 定义一些超参数，衰减率设为零点九，学习率设为零点零零一。

这跟我们之前在 TensorFlow 版本里的设置是一样的，在那个版本中这些步骤都是自动完成的。

再接下来，我们为 RMSProp 中的每个变量定义更新规则。

所以，我们有缓存值，它被初始化成一个跟各参数维度相同的全壹数组。

我们定义新的缓存值，也就是缓存值在每一步中应该怎样被更新。

然后，我们把 RMSProp 的更新过程定义为一个由元组构成的列表。

下一步，我们根据刚才构建好的这些，来定义几个可调用的函数。

于是，我们有一个 train_op，它接收一个 X，返回代价，并执行 RMSProp 更新。

接着是 predict 函数，它接收一个 X，返回重建值 X_hat。

然后，我们定义一个 fit 函数。

它接收 X，也就是训练数据、epochs 的数量以及批处理大小。

在这个函数内部，我们通过循环来遍历所有 epochs 和 batches，在每个 epoch 中都会打乱数据，并在每个 batch 上调用我们之前定义好的 train_op。

我们会把代价都存下来，这样结束后就能绘制出相应的曲线。

这个函数之所以这么短，是因为大部分工作我们都在构造器里做完了。

请注意，我们不必像在 TensorFlow 里那样再专门定义一个 predict 函数，因为 Theano 的 function 函数返回的结果本身就是可调用的。

接下来是主函数。

它会加载数据，创建一个隐藏层大小为三百的自编码器，将数据喂给它进行拟合，然后在一个循环里，不断随机生成重建图像。

这里的设计是，你可以敲击字母 n 键退出，或者按回车或其他任意键来继续生成随机的重建结果。

那么，让我们运行一下看看结果如何。

Hey everyone and welcome back to this class, Unsupervised Deep Learning, Part 2.

In this lecture, we are going to implement an autoencoder in Theano as a coding warm-up.

If you don't want to try to code this yourself, although I strongly recommend you do, the relevant file in the course repo is autoencoderthiano.py.

At the top, we have all our usual imports, numpy, matplotlib, theano, and util for getting the data.

Next, we define our autoencoder class, which will be a one-hidden layer neural network with a rel u activation function and sigmoid output, since images only have values between 0 and 1.

Most of the work is done in the constructor, so you want to look here for the relevant details of the file.

So we start out by creating a placeholder for the input data x.

This is going to be a 2D matrix of size n by d, but we don't need to specify the exact shape.

Next, we define the input to hidden weights and bias.

These weights are d by m, where d is the input dimensionality and m is the hidden layer dimensionality.

Next, we define the hidden to output weights and bias.

These weights are the opposite shape, so they take you from m back to d in order to reconstruct the input.

Next, we join all these variables together by creating an expression for the output x hat.

So for the cost function, you could use squared error and consider the difference between your inputs and outputs to be Gaussian, or you could use cross entropy like we did in the previous unsupervised deep learning course and assume your inputs and outputs to be Bernoulli probabilities.

We are doing the latter, but you should feel free to try squared error as well.

Next, we set up the training procedure.

The first step is to collect all our params in a list.

Then we can find the gradient of the cost with respect to each of these params. Next, we define some hyperparameters for RMSProp, a decay of 0.9 and a learning rate of 0.001.

This will match what we have in the TensorFlow version, which does all this work for us.

Next, we define the updates for each variable in RMSProp.

So we have the cache, which is initialized to a ones array, the same size as each param.

We define new cache, which is what the cache should be updated to on each step.

And then we define the RMS prop updates as a list of tuples.

Next, we define some callable functions based on what we just built.

So we have a train op that takes an X, returns the cost, and performs the RMS prop updates.

Then we have predict, which takes an X and returns X hat, the reconstruction.

Next, we define a fit function.

This takes an X, the training data, number of epochs, and the batch size.

Inside this function, we loop through the number of epochs and the number of batches, shuffle the data over each epoch, and call the train op we defined earlier on each batch.

We save the costs so we can plot them when we're done.

This function is short because we did most of the work in the constructor.

Notice how we don't have to define a separate predict function like we did with TensorFlow, because the Fiano function function returns a callable function.

Next, we have the main function.

This loads in the data, creates an autoencoder with hidden layer size 300, fits it to the data, and then generates random reconstructions in a loop.

I've designed this so you can hit the letter N to quit, or just hit Enter or any other character to continue generating random reconstructions.

So let's run this and see what we get.
